[TOOL CALL run_python]
import numpy as np
def dihedral_transforms():
    fns={
     'id':lambda x:x,
     'r90':lambda x:np.rot90(x,1),      # CCW
     'r180':lambda x:np.rot90(x,2),
     'r270':lambda x:np.rot90(x,3),     # CW
     'flipud':lambda x:np.flipud(x),
     'fliplr':lambda x:np.fliplr(x),
     'T':lambda x:x.T,
     'antiT':lambda x:np.fliplr(np.rot90(x,1)),  # fliplr_r90
    }
    return fns
fns=dihedral_transforms()

# main color for each example: pick color with max count that isn't 0
def analyze(gi,go):
    vals,counts=np.unique(gi,return_counts=True)
    # main color likely the one whose bbox matches output (rotated dims)
    res={}
    for c in vals:
        if c==0: continue
        idx=np.argwhere(gi==c)
        r0,c0=idx.min(0); r1,c1=idx.max(0)
        reg=gi[r0:r1+1,c0:c1+1]
        for name,f in fns.items():
            if f(reg).shape==go.shape and np.array_equal(f(reg),go):
                res[c]=(name,(r0,r1,c0,c1),reg)
    return res

for i in range(3):
    gi=np.array(train[i]['input']); go=np.array(train[i]['output'])
    print("ex",i, analyze(gi,go).keys())
    for c,v in analyze(gi,go).items():
        print("  color",c,"transform",v[0],"bbox",v[1])

[TOOL CALL run_python]
def features(gi, mainc):
    idx=np.argwhere(gi==mainc)
    r0,r1=idx[:,0].min(),idx[:,0].max()
    c0,c1=idx[:,1].min(),idx[:,1].max()
    H,W=gi.shape
    sides={}
    # left: col c0-1 rows r0..r1
    if c0-1>=0:
        col=gi[r0:r1+1,c0-1]
        sides['left']=col
    if c1+1<W:
        sides['right']=gi[r0:r1+1,c1+1]
    if r0-1>=0:
        sides['top']=gi[r0-1,c0:c1+1]
    if r1+1<H:
        sides['bottom']=gi[r1+1,c0:c1+1]
    return (r0,r1,c0,c1),sides

for i in range(3):
    gi=np.array(train[i]['input'])
    mainc=[1,2,8][i]
    bb,sides=features(gi,mainc)
    print("ex",i,"bbox",bb)
    for k,v in sides.items():
        vals,cnt=np.unique(v,return_counts=True)
        print("   ",k,dict(zip(vals.tolist(),cnt.tolist())))

Now the outer edges: Let me code to find for each strip color, which outer edge is dominated by that color.
[TOOL CALL run_python]
def outer_edge(gi,color):
    H,W=gi.shape
    edges={}
    edges['top']=gi[0,:]
    edges['bottom']=gi[H-1,:]
    edges['left']=gi[:,0]
    edges['right']=gi[:,W-1]
    best=[]
    for k,v in edges.items():
        frac=(v==color).mean()
        best.append((k,round(frac,2)))
    best.sort(key=lambda x:-x[1])
    return best

for i in range(3):
    gi=np.array(train[i]['input'])
    mainc=[1,2,8][i]
    bb,sides=features(gi,mainc)
    print("ex",i)
    for k,v in sides.items():
        vals,cnt=np.unique(v,return_counts=True)
        if len(vals)==1 and vals[0]!=0:
            col=vals[0]
            print("   strip side",k,"color",col,"outer edges:",outer_edge(gi,col))

def border(gi):
    H,W=gi.shape
    top=gi[0,:].tolist()
    right=gi[1:,W-1].tolist()
    bottom=gi[H-1,W-2::-1].tolist()
    left=gi[H-2:0:-1,0].tolist()
    return top+right+bottom+left
for i in range(3):
    gi=np.array(train[i]['input'])
    b=border(gi)
    print("ex",i, len(b))
    print(b)
ti=np.array(test_input)
print("test", len(border(ti)))
print(border(ti))

[TOOL CALL run_python]
def border_info(gi):
    H,W=gi.shape
    seq=[]
    # top row left->right
    seq+= [('top',c,int(gi[0,c])) for c in range(W)]
    seq+= [('right',r,int(gi[r,W-1])) for r in range(1,H)]
    seq+= [('bottom',c,int(gi[H-1,c])) for c in range(W-2,-1,-1)]
    seq+= [('left',r,int(gi[r,0])) for r in range(H-2,0,-1)]
    return seq
for i in range(3):
    gi=np.array(train[i]['input'])
    seq=border_info(gi)
    # values
    vals=[v for _,_,v in seq]
    print("ex",i,"len",len(vals))
    # find runs
    runs=[]
    cur=vals[0]; cnt=1; start=0
    for k in range(1,len(vals)):
        if vals[k]==cur: cnt+=1
        else:
            runs.append((cur,cnt,start)); cur=vals[k]; cnt=1; start=k
    runs.append((cur,cnt,start))
    print(runs)

[TOOL CALL run_python]
def get_noise(gi):
    H,W=gi.shape
    seq=[]
    seq+= [int(gi[0,c]) for c in range(W)]
    seq+= [int(gi[r,W-1]) for r in range(1,H)]
    seq+= [int(gi[H-1,c]) for c in range(W-2,-1,-1)]
    seq+= [int(gi[r,0]) for r in range(H-2,0,-1)]
    # noise cells are those in {0,5}
    # find contiguous region of noise
    noise_idx=[k for k,v in enumerate(seq) if v in (0,5)]
    # they should form a contiguous cyclic block
    return seq,noise_idx

for i in range(3):
    gi=np.array(train[i]['input'])
    seq,ni=get_noise(gi)
    print("ex",i)
    print(" noise idx", ni[0], ni[-1], "count",len(ni))
    bits=[1 if seq[k]==5 else 0 for k in ni]
    print(" bits", ''.join(map(str,bits)))
ti=np.array(test_input)
seq,ni=get_noise(ti)
print("test noise idx",ni[0],ni[-1],"count",len(ni))
print(" bits", ''.join('1' if seq[k]==5 else '0' for k in ni))

Now here's a thought: The transform T to get the output might be: "rotate the object so that its band-corner goes to a canonical position AND the frame corner goes to a canonical position." Since frame corner and band corner have a definite relation, both are determined by the same rotation (if rotation) or reflection.

For a rotation T, the relative position between frame corner and band corner (as seen from the object) is preserved. For a reflection, it's mirrored. So the "diagonal vs adjacent" property is a rigid invariant—it can't distinguish rotation/reflection! Because a rotation of a diagonal relation is still diagonal; reflection also gives diagonal. So ex0/test diagonal and ex1/ex2 adjacent means the INPUTS have different intrinsic relations, not different transforms. So the transform can't be determined by matching a fixed canonical relation. Let me try to settle by thinking about the actual generative program in ARC. Such tasks are often generated by code like:
1. Create a random shape S (the region content).
2. Apply a random D4 transform g to S → S'.
3. Place S' on canvas.
4. Draw borders: for the object, add colored strips on the sides that correspond to g applied to fixed sides (e.g., bottom and left); color the frame edges correspondingly.
